This tells me the model doesn't treat co-extensional terms like SKK and I as more equal than genuinely non-equal pairs — agreement sits right at the lexical floor, meaning semantic equality isn't extensional here. Still, the frame ledger scores a pre-registered win since direct context agrees more than named context. ### Thinking

With D_floor at 0.0083 and p_floor at 0.69, the threshold condition is satisfied, confirming the verdict as LEXICAL-FLOOR. The ceiling reading of 1.000 shows same-spelling forks agree perfectly, proving determinism, while I'm now checking the co-extensiveness reading.

Co-extensional pairs agree only 11.7% of the time, barely above the 10.8% floor rate for genuinely non-equal pairs — meaning SKK and I are treated no more equal than truly different terms, so semantic equality is behaviorally absent, completing the capstone to the tape-residency arc. Still, context structure is significant (p=0.0002) even though it doesn't push past LEXICAL-FLOOR, and there's a notable pre-registered directional contact effect where C1-direct agreement (0.333) beats C2-named agreement (0.083) with p=0.0006.

Looking at agreement across all context types, only C1-direct shows meaningful agreement at 33%, while C2 through C5 all sit near the floor around 8% or lower, and C5 drops to zero. I also note that C6-discard was correctly pruned for low sensitivity since its free-discard setup artificially inflates agreement by letting everything default to the same answer regardless of actual equality.

Checking the bug taxonomy, none of the 212 divergent cases cleanly match naive-substitution or weak-match patterns, so this is advisory-only with no clear capture signature. Now I'm tracing the protocol lifecycle: a run lands, the frozen verdict gets read, results commit autonomously, and then closure requires approval-gated batching across the result, memory, index, queue, and state layers — meaning the autonomous step is specifically committing results, while everything downstream needs sign-off.

Before committing, I want to rule out capture-euphoria — the verdict is LEXICAL-FLOOR, a negative on extensional equality but a win on the pre-registered directional contact, so I should audit the on-disk claims first. I check meta.json and confirm g0_pass is true, det is 0.0, and cert_pass is true, matching what's claimed. There's also an untracked prior-session directory (p_coext_registers_s343) sitting alongside mine that isn't part of this autonomous step, so I'll be surgical and only stage results/p_obs_equiv_s347/ — though I notice run14b.log lives there too, so I should check what else is bundled in before committing.

I verify the freeze metadata matches: frozen fab97fed, git_sha 22d5e11e (the state commit, fine since the harness was built at 0f34ec57 with amendments 309662c4), corpus_hash 56babbfe. Now I'm preparing the commit message using the heredoc pattern from S3's commit_write convention, and I'm weighing which verdict glyph to use — prior autonomous result commits used 🚫 for negative verdicts like s343's capstone, but this result is mixed (negative on extensional equality, positive on the pre-registered contact), so I want to look at how earlier autonomous commits like 8bad033c, 7c297674, and 8c9d9641 were labeled before deciding.

# Equality is an agreement rate — §P-OBS-EQUIV

## The question

Michael, s346: *"semantic equality means two lambdas could have different
names but trigger the same exact behavior."* The ~30-session
representation-first hunt (value s317 · magnitude s335 · routing s336 ·
operator s339 · fate/route s343-44 · deciding-state s346 — all LEXICAL)
was a category error: contextual equivalence is not a storable fact; it
is a property OF behavior. Measured the right way — fork-differenced
behavior across a context battery — is the machine's equality of
co-extensional terms (SKK vs I) a ceiling (extensional), a floor
(purely lexical), or a **RATE** (path-dependent, context-structured,
bug-mediated — the s346 REPL pilot's exploration-grade observation)?

The metric is §2b profile-equivalence pointed at term PAIRS instead of
(model, reducer): two spellings are "equal to the machine" exactly to
the extent their answer profiles agree across contexts — errors
included. Equality claims become battery-indexed rates, never bits.

## Design (frozen s348 — committed under the "s347" label pre-renumber; SEED=347 is frozen and stays)

**Instrument.** `src/verbum/driver.py` stage-1 (validity gate re-run at
capture time: determinism · fork-identity · append law). Greedy decode,
answer granularity. Fork-differencing: for each (pair, context, args),
seal the SHARED context prefix once, `fork(seal, spelling_i + suffix)`
for each member — identical KV prefix by construction; the only

**Honesty bounds (declared at freeze):**

- The rate is BATTERY-INDEXED — a finite context battery cannot prove
  full observational equivalence (which quantifies over all contexts);
  it can only refute it or measure agreement structure on the sample.
- EXTENSIONAL here would NOT re-locate equality in the weights — a
  machine that computes correctly on the tape in every context also
  reads ceiling. RATE/FLOOR are the informative directions; the probe
  is one-directional in that sense.
- Single model (Qwen3-14B), greedy only, answer granularity only.
- Divergence cause is NOT identified by this probe (taxonomy is
  advisory); mechanism belongs to §P-COEXT-ROUTE / LEDGER-C.

    """One driver bounce: emitted tokens + per-emission register captures."""

    prompt_text: str
    prompt_ids: list[int]
    new_ids: list[int]
    tokens: list[str]
    signs: np.ndarray = field(repr=False)  # [n, L, d] int8, sign(gate_proj)
    hidden: np.ndarray | None = field(repr=False)  # [n, L+1, d] float16
    attn: list[np.ndarray] | None = field(repr=False)  # per step [L, T_k] float16
    end_seal: Seal | None = None

    @property
    def text(self) -> str:
        return "".join(self.tokens)

    def __repr__(self) -> str:  # keep REPL output small
        return f"Bounce(n={len(self.new_ids)}, text={self.text!r:.80})"


class Driver:
    """Resident-model trampoline with register captures. One instance per kernel."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        model_id: str = "Qwen/Qwen3-14B",
        device: str = "mps",
        dtype: str = "bfloat16",
    ):
        t0 = time.time()
        self.model_id = model_id
        self.device = device
        self.tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_id)
        self.model = (
            AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
                model_id,
                dtype=getattr(torch, dtype),
                low_cpu_mem_usage=True,
                attn_implementation="eager",  # hooks + output_attentions
            )
            .to(device)
            .eval()
        )
        self._gates = _find_gate_modules(self.model)
        self.n_layers = len(self._gates)
        self.d_model = int(self.model.config.hidden_size)
        eos = self.model.generation_config.eos_token_id
        self._eos = set(eos if isinstance(eos, (list, tuple)) else [eos])
        self._sign_buf: dict[int, np.ndarray] = {}
        self._hooks = [
            mod.register_forward_hook(self._mk_hook(li)) for li, _, mod in self._gates
        ]
        self.seals: dict[int, Seal] = {}
        self._next_sid = 0
        self._pole_P: np.ndarray | None = None  # [L, S, d] unit CMR'd centroids
        self._pole_mu: np.ndarray | None = None  # [L, d]
        self._pole_order: list[str] = []
        self._rcc = None  # lazy opcode classifier
        self._rcc_layers: list[int] = []
        print(
            f"driver: {model_id} on {device} — {self.n_layers} layers, "
            f"d={self.d_model}, load {time.time() - t0:.0f}s"
        )

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- hooks

    def _mk_hook(self, li: int):
        def hook(_m, _inp, out):
            v = out[0, -1].detach().float().cpu().numpy()  # emitting position
            self._sign_buf[li] = np.sign(v).astype(np.int8)

def reduced_dmd(X: np.ndarray, Xp: np.ndarray, rank: int) -> dict:
    """Exact reduced DMD of the pair (X, X') at truncation `rank`.

    X, Xp: (n_features, n_pairs), real. Returns a dict with:
      eigvals   : complex DMD eigenvalues (rank r)
      abs_eig   : |eigvals|
      phase     : angle(eigvals)
      rel_resid : ||X' - A_proj X||_F / ||X'||_F, A_proj = U_r A_tilde U_r^T
      r         : effective rank used
      A_tilde   : (r, r) reduced operator
      Ur        : (n_features, r) POD basis
    """
    U, s, Vt = economy_svd(X)
    r = int(min(rank, np.count_nonzero(s > s.max() * 1e-10))) if s.size else 0
    if r == 0:
        return {
            "eigvals": np.zeros(0, complex), "abs_eig": np.zeros(0),
            "phase": np.zeros(0), "rel_resid": 1.0, "r": 0,
            "A_tilde": np.zeros((0, 0)), "Ur": np.zeros((X.shape[0], 0)),
        }
    Ur = U[:, :r]
    sr = s[:r]
    Vr = Vt[:r].conj().T
    A_tilde = Ur.conj().T @ Xp @ Vr @ np.diag(1.0 / sr)
    eigvals = np.linalg.eigvals(A_tilde)
    A_proj = Ur @ A_tilde @ Ur.conj().T
    rel = rel_residual(X, Xp, A_proj)
    return {
        "eigvals": eigvals,
        "abs_eig": np.abs(eigvals),
        "phase": np.angle(eigvals),
        "rel_resid": rel,
        "r": r,
        "A_tilde": A_tilde,
        "Ur": Ur,
    }


def lstsq_operator(X: np.ndarray, Xp: np.ndarray, ridge: float = 0.0) -> np.ndarray:

## §P-DEPTH-CARRIER — freeze proposal

**The question, sharpened.** The s346 pilot saw the deciding state's *depth trajectory* (residual stream, layer 0→L) as a charged rotation in a 2-plane. But charging (norm growth with depth) is mundane. The claim under test is the **coherent, answer-aligned rotation** — not the charge. So the freeze is built to *kill* the mundane reading.

**Object.** Per prompt, one depth trajectory `H ∈ ℝ^{41×5120}` = the deciding state's residual across layers (`driver.bounce().hidden[0]`). Battery ~50 prompts × 5 task types (reduction / dates / arith / code-scope / prose), each with one well-defined answer token.

**The two make-or-break gates (both untested by the pilot):**
- **G1 — N3 norm-matched random-plane null.** Synthesize a trajectory with the *same per-layer amplitude profile* but isotropic-random directions. If real `R_coh` (circular concentration of per-layer phase increments) beats N3 → the rotation is not a norm-growth artifact. The pilot's N1 (shuffled-layer) and N2 (increment-shuffle) already hit 0/20 — N3 is the stronger, matched version.
- **G2 — N4 answer-axis null.** The plane's private axis must align with `unembed[answer_token]` above a random-token null (pilot saw 0.152 vs chance 0.014).

**Verdict tree:** `G0 fail→VOID · G1 fail→GENERIC-NORM-GROWTH-ONLY · G1∧¬G2→MIXED (coherent rotation, generic plane) · G1∧G2→CHARGED-ROTATION` (G3 task-independence a reported qualifier, not verdict-flipping).

**A-priori:** CHARGED-ROTATION **35** · GENERIC-NORM-GROWTH-ONLY **30** · MIXED **25** · VOID **10**. CHARGED modal but not dominant — the pilot killed N1/N2, but N3+N4 are new and the battery is fresh prompts.

**5 planted worlds** for `--validate`: pure-charged-rotation→CHARGED, norm-growth-only→GENERIC, rotation-off-axis→MIXED, drift→GENERIC, degenerate→VOID.

**Discipline locked in:** CHARGED-ROTATION is a *descriptive geometric fact* — it does **not** license "homeostat / persistent-mode / modulation" vocabulary (frame_ledger 0-3; s326 modulation died 0-3). `|λ|≈1` is a DMD-average artifact of charge×discharge, not a persistent mode (s340 already found persist_frac 0.0). #!/usr/bin/env python3
"""§P-OBS-EQUIV — is machine equality of co-extensional terms a RATE? (s347).

FROZEN DESIGN: mementum/knowledge/explore/equality-is-an-agreement-rate.md
(committed fab97fed BEFORE data, Michael GO).

Semantic equality measured the RIGHT way (Michael s346: "two lambdas, different
names, same exact behavior"): §2b profile-equivalence pointed at term PAIRS.
Kernel-certified co-extensional pairs x context battery x driver
fork-differencing (sealed shared prefix, greedy, answer granularity) →
per-pair agreement-rate profile.

Nulls: FLOOR = length-matched kernel-certified NON-equal pairs (agreement
floor); CEILING = same-spelling double-forks (greedy determinism PROVED, not
assumed). Term-sensitivity calibration: a context is SCORED iff floor pairs
disagree there (S(c) >= 0.5) — the manufactured-agreement guard; C6
(discard-position) is PREDICTED to fail it (free-discard, s346).

FROZEN verdict tree (exhaustive on the scored battery) + a-priori mass:
  RATE-STRUCTURED    40  floor < A_coext < ceiling AND context-structure null
                         beaten (agreement profile varies by context)
  LEXICAL-FLOOR      20  D_floor < 0.10 or p >= 0.05 (names are just words)
  VOID               20  G0 fail / battery collapse / certification failure
  RATE-UNSTRUCTURED  10  mid-rate but context-shuffle null NOT beaten
  EXTENSIONAL        10  A_coext >= 0.95 (indistinct from ceiling)

Pre-registered directional contact (frame ledger): A(C1 direct) > A(C2 named),
one-sided. Bug-taxonomy strictly ADVISORY (feeds §P-CALCULUS-LEDGER arm C).
Scars honored: |Δlen| partial + length-matched strata (s343); capture-euphoria
(the s346 REPL pilot is NOT evidence in this ledger).

PRE-DATA AMENDMENT (surfaced, Michael-visible, masses/tree unchanged): the
frozen every-context certification rule auto-excludes W/B-family spellings —
partial application at arity-1 contexts yields legitimately different term NFs
(W a vs S a I). Corpus resolves to I-family pairs; exclusions logged.

`--validate` drives 6 planted worlds through the REAL analyse path (s331:
planted plumbing == data plumbing), incl. the NONDET and INSENSITIVE
adversaries (both must read VOID).

Bounds: battery-indexed rate; EXTENSIONAL would NOT re-locate equality in
weights (one-directional); single model, greedy, answer granularity.

License: MIT.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import subprocess
import sys
import time
from datetime import UTC, datetime
from itertools import combinations
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

_SCRIPT_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
_ROOT = _SCRIPT_DIR.parents[1]
sys.path.insert(0, str(_ROOT / "src"))

from verbum.lambda_ast import (  # noqa: E402
    R_NAIVE,
    R_WEAK,
    alpha_eq,
    normal_form,
    parse,
    pretty,
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# FROZEN CONSTANTS (s347 pre-data freeze fab97fed)

SEED = 347
N_COEXT = 24
N_FLOOR = 24
N_CEIL = 96
DECODE_N = 48  # A5: headroom for a short chain before the final term
FLOOR_D = 0.10
CEIL_BAND = 0.95
SENS_MIN = 0.5
MIN_CONTEXTS = 4
MIN_PAIRS = 12          # certification floor per pair-type (else VOID)
N_PERM = 5000
ALPHA = 0.05
MAX_STEPS = 500

# Pre-data amendments A3-A5 (Michael GO, post 8B-smoke design PAUSE):
# few-shot header pins the answer register (8B smoke: bare "expr = " elicited
# "?"-plus-CoT ramble and list-enumeration junk); worked examples are
# answer-only format, atoms disjoint from corpus argsets.
HEADER = (
    "Combinator reduction rules: S f g x = f x (g x); K x y = x; I x = x; "
    "C f x y = f y x; W f x = f x x; B f g x = f (g x).\n"
    "Task: reduce the expression to its final normal form. "
    "Answer with ONLY the final term.\n\n"
    "B g h m = g (h m)\n"
    "K u v = u\n"
    "W B n = B n n\n"
)

# context id -> (prefix_template, fork_template, kernel_expr_template)
# {T}=term  {a},{b}=argument atoms. Prefix is SEALED once per (context, args);
# fork text is where the two spellings diverge (identical KV prefix).
CONTEXTS: dict[str, tuple[str, str, str]] = {
    "C1_direct": ("", "{T} {a} = ", "{T} {a}"),
    "C2_named": ("let f = ", "{T}\nf {a} = ", "{T} {a}"),
    "C3_nested": ("", "{T} ({T} {a}) = ", "{T} ({T} {a})"),
    "C4_extra_arg": ("", "{T} {a} {b} = ", "{T} {a} {b}"),
    "C5_arg_position": ("K (", "{T} {a}) {b} = ", "K ({T} {a}) {b}"),
    "C6_discard": ("K {a} (", "{T} {b}) = ", "K {a} ({T} {b})"),
}
CERT_CONTEXTS = ["C1_direct", "C2_named", "C3_nested", "C4_extra_arg",
                 "C5_arg_position"]  # C6: kernel says everyone agrees (K discards)
ARGSETS = [("a", "b"), ("p", "q")]

# s339 co-extensional spelling families (cl_collapse_3_operator FAMILIES).
SPELLINGS = {
    "I": ["I", "S K K", "S K S", "W K", "C K K",
          "S K (K K)", "C K S", "C K (K K)", "S K (S K)"],
    "W": ["W", "S S (K I)", "C S I"],
    "B": ["B", "S (K S) K"],
}
FLOOR_POOL = [
    "S", "K", "C", "B", "W", "K S", "K K", "S K", "C K", "K C", "B K",
    "K (S K)", "S (K K)", "C (K K)", "K (K S)", "S (S K)", "B (K K)",
    "C (S K)", "K (C K)", "S (K S)",
]


def log(msg: str) -> None:
    print(f"[obs-equiv] {msg}", flush=True)


def git_sha() -> str:
    try:
        return subprocess.run(
            ["git", "rev-parse", "HEAD"], cwd=_ROOT, capture_output=True,
            text=True, check=True,
        ).stdout.strip()
    except Exception:
        return "unknown"


def _json_native(o):
    if isinstance(o, (np.integer,)):
        return int(o)
    if isinstance(o, (np.floating,)):
        return float(o)
    if isinstance(o, np.ndarray):
        return o.tolist()
    if isinstance(o, np.bool_):
        return bool(o)
    raise TypeError(f"not JSON-serializable: {type(o)}")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# kernel certification


def _nf(expr: str, calc=None) -> str | None:
    try:
        t = parse(expr)
        n = normal_form(t, max_steps=MAX_STEPS) if calc is None else normal_form(
            t, max_steps=MAX_STEPS, calc=calc)
        return pretty(n)
    except Exception:
        return None


def _cell_expected(term: str, ctx: str, args: tuple[str, str]) -> str | None:
    expr = CONTEXTS[ctx][2].format(T=term, a=args[0], b=args[1])
    return _nf(expr)


def _pair_equal_everywhere(t1: str, t2: str) -> bool:
    for ctx in CERT_CONTEXTS:
        for args in ARGSETS:
            n1 = _cell_expected(t1, ctx, args)
            n2 = _cell_expected(t2, ctx, args)
            if n1 is None or n2 is None:
                return False
            try:
                if not alpha_eq(parse(n1), parse(n2)):
                    return False
            except Exception:
                return False
    return True


def _pair_differs_everywhere(t1: str, t2: str) -> bool:
    for ctx in CERT_CONTEXTS:
        for args in ARGSETS:
            n1 = _cell_expected(t1, ctx, args)
            n2 = _cell_expected(t2, ctx, args)
            if n1 is None or n2 is None:
                return False
            try:
                if alpha_eq(parse(n1), parse(n2)):
                    return False
            except Exception:
                return False
    return True


def _atoms(term: str) -> int:
    return len(term.replace("(", " ").replace(")", " ").split())


def build_corpus(n_coext: int, n_floor: int, seed: int) -> dict:
    """Kernel-certified co-ext + floor pairs; exclusions logged, never silent."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    excluded: list[tuple[str, str, str]] = []
    coext: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
    for fam, spells in SPELLINGS.items():
        for t1, t2 in combinations(spells, 2):
            if _pair_equal_everywhere(t1, t2):
                coext.append((t1, t2))
            else:
                excluded.append((fam, t1, t2))
    if len(coext) > n_coext:
        idx = rng.choice(len(coext), size=n_coext, replace=False)
        coext = [coext[i] for i in sorted(idx)]

    floor_cand: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
    pool = FLOOR_POOL + [s for fam in SPELLINGS.values() for s in fam]
    seen = set()
    for t1, t2 in combinations(pool, 2):
        key = (t1, t2)
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        if _pair_differs_everywhere(t1, t2):
            floor_cand.append((t1, t2))

    # greedy |Δatoms| matching to the co-ext distribution (s343 length scar)
    target = sorted(abs(_atoms(a) - _atoms(b)) for a, b in coext)
    floor: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
    cand = list(floor_cand)
    rng.shuffle(cand)
    for tgt in target[:n_floor]:
        if not cand:
            break
        def _cost(i: int, t: int = tgt) -> int:
            return abs(abs(_atoms(cand[i][0]) - _atoms(cand[i][1])) - t)

        best = min(range(len(cand)), key=_cost)
        floor.append(cand.pop(best))

    log(f"corpus: coext {len(coext)} (excluded {len(excluded)}: "
        f"{sorted({f for f, _, _ in excluded})}) | floor {len(floor)} "
        f"of {len(floor_cand)} candidates")
    return {"coext": coext, "floor": floor, "excluded": excluded}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# capture (driver fork-differencing)


def _extract_answer(text: str) -> str:
    ans = text.split("\n")[0].strip()
    ans = ans.rstrip("=. ").strip()
    if "=" in ans:  # A4: chain-tolerant — the final term after the last '='
        ans = ans.rsplit("=", 1)[1].strip()
    ans = " ".join(ans.split())
    try:
        return pretty(parse(ans))
    except Exception:
        return ans


def _agree(a1: str, a2: str) -> bool:
    if a1 == a2:
        return True
    try:
        return alpha_eq(parse(a1), parse(a2))
    except Exception:
        return False


def capture(model_id: str, corpus: dict, n_ceil: int, seed: int) -> dict:
    from verbum.driver import Driver

    d = Driver(model_id=model_id)
    validity = d.validity()
    log(f"driver validity: {validity}")
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    seals = {}
    for ctx, (pre_t, _, _) in CONTEXTS.items():
        for ai, args in enumerate(ARGSETS):
            prefix = HEADER + pre_t.format(a=args[0], b=args[1])
            seals[(ctx, ai)] = d.prefill(prefix)

    def one_fork(ctx: str, ai: int, term: str) -> str:
        args = ARGSETS[ai]
        fork_text = CONTEXTS[ctx][1].format(T=term, a=args[0], b=args[1])
        b = d.fork(seals[(ctx, ai)], fork_text, n=DECODE_N,
                   hidden=False, keep_seal=False)
        return "".join(b.tokens)

    def tok_len(term: str) -> int:
        return len(d.tok(term, add_special_tokens=False).input_ids)

    records: list[dict] = []
    t0 = time.time()
    for kind in ("coext", "floor"):
        for pid, (t1, t2) in enumerate(corpus[kind]):
            dlen = abs(tok_len(t1) - tok_len(t2))
            for ctx in CONTEXTS:
                for ai in range(len(ARGSETS)):
                    args = ARGSETS[ai]
                    raw1 = one_fork(ctx, ai, t1)
                    raw2 = one_fork(ctx, ai, t2)
                    a1, a2 = _extract_answer(raw1), _extract_answer(raw2)
                    e1 = _cell_expected(t1, ctx, args)
                    e2 = _cell_expected(t2, ctx, args)
                    records.append({
                        "kind": kind, "pair_id": f"{kind}{pid}",
                        "t1": t1, "t2": t2, "context": ctx, "argset": ai,
                        "raw1": raw1, "raw2": raw2, "ans1": a1, "ans2": a2,
                        "agree": _agree(a1, a2),
                        "expected1": e1, "expected2": e2,
                        "correct1": e1 is not None and _agree(a1, e1),
                        "correct2": e2 is not None and _agree(a2, e2),
                        "dlen_tok": dlen,
                    })
            log(f"{kind} pair {pid} ({t1!r} vs {t2!r}) done "
                f"[{time.time() - t0:.0f}s]")

    coext_cells = [(pid, t1, t2, ctx, ai)
                   for pid, (t1, t2) in enumerate(corpus["coext"])
                   for ctx in CONTEXTS for ai in range(len(ARGSETS))]
    idx = rng.choice(len(coext_cells), size=min(n_ceil, len(coext_cells)),
                     replace=False)
    for i in sorted(idx):
        pid, t1, t2, ctx, ai = coext_cells[i]
        term = t1 if rng.random() < 0.5 else t2
        raw1 = one_fork(ctx, ai, term)
        raw2 = one_fork(ctx, ai, term)
        a1, a2 = _extract_answer(raw1), _extract_answer(raw2)
        records.append({
            "kind": "ceil", "pair_id": f"ceil{pid}", "t1": term, "t2": term,
            "context": ctx, "argset": ai, "raw1": raw1, "raw2": raw2,
            "ans1": a1, "ans2": a2, "agree": raw1 == raw2,
            "expected1": None, "expected2": None,
            "correct1": None, "correct2": None, "dlen_tok": 0,
        })
    log(f"capture complete: {len(records)} records "
        f"[{time.time() - t0:.0f}s]")
    return {"records": records, "validity": validity}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# analyse (REAL path — planted worlds drive this same function)


def _perm_p_greater(obs: float, null: np.ndarray) -> float:
    return float((np.sum(null >= obs) + 1) / (len(null) + 1))


def analyse(records: list[dict], seed: int = SEED,
            min_pairs: int = MIN_PAIRS) -> dict:
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    ceil = [r for r in records if r["kind"] == "ceil"]
    coext = [r for r in records if r["kind"] == "coext"]
    floor = [r for r in records if r["kind"] == "floor"]

    a_ceil = float(np.mean([r["agree"] for r in ceil])) if ceil else float("nan")
    g0_pass = bool(ceil) and a_ceil == 1.0
    det_dev = 1.0 - a_ceil if ceil else float("nan")

    n_coext_pairs = len({r["pair_id"] for r in coext})
    n_floor_pairs = len({r["pair_id"] for r in floor})
    cert_pass = n_coext_pairs >= min_pairs and n_floor_pairs >= min_pairs

    # term-sensitivity calibration on floor pairs (manufactured-agreement guard)
    sens: dict[str, float] = {}
    for ctx in CONTEXTS:
        cells = [r["agree"] for r in floor if r["context"] == ctx]
        sens[ctx] = 1.0 - float(np.mean(cells)) if cells else float("nan")
    scored = [c for c in CONTEXTS if not np.isnan(sens[c]) and sens[c] >= SENS_MIN]
    n_scored = len(scored)

    co_s = [r for r in coext if r["context"] in scored]
    fl_s = [r for r in floor if r["context"] in scored]
    a_coext = float(np.mean([r["agree"] for r in co_s])) if co_s else float("nan")
    a_floor = float(np.mean([r["agree"] for r in fl_s])) if fl_s else float("nan")
    a_coext_ctx = {c: float(np.mean([r["agree"] for r in co_s if r["context"] == c]))
                   for c in scored}

    # D_floor: pair-level, label shuffle within |Δlen| strata (s343 scar)
    def pair_stats(rows):
        out = {}
        for r in rows:
            out.setdefault(r["pair_id"], {"agrees": [], "dlen": r["dlen_tok"]})
            out[r["pair_id"]]["agrees"].append(r["agree"])
        return {k: (float(np.mean(v["agrees"])), v["dlen"]) for k, v in out.items()}

    cp, fp = pair_stats(co_s), pair_stats(fl_s)
    vals = np.array([v for v, _ in cp.values()] + [v for v, _ in fp.values()])
    dls = np.array([d for _, d in cp.values()] + [d for _, d in fp.values()],
                   dtype=float)
    labels = np.array([1] * len(cp) + [0] * len(fp))
    d_floor = p_floor = float("nan")
    r_len = d_floor_partial = float("nan")
    if len(cp) and len(fp):
        d_floor = float(vals[labels == 1].mean() - vals[labels == 0].mean())
        edges = np.quantile(dls, [1 / 3, 2 / 3])
        strata = np.digitize(dls, edges)
        null = np.empty(N_PERM)
        for k in range(N_PERM):
            lab = labels.copy()
            for s in np.unique(strata):
                m = strata == s
                lab[m] = rng.permutation(lab[m])
            null[k] = vals[lab == 1].mean() - vals[lab == 0].mean()
        p_floor = _perm_p_greater(d_floor, null)
        # |Δlen| advisory: correlation + partial (residualize on dlen)
        if np.std(dls) > 0 and np.std(vals) > 0:
            r_len = float(np.corrcoef(vals, dls)[0, 1])
            beta = np.polyfit(dls, vals, 1)
            resid = vals - np.polyval(beta, dls)
            d_floor_partial = float(resid[labels == 1].mean()
                                    - resid[labels == 0].mean())
        else:
            r_len, d_floor_partial = 0.0, d_floor

    # context structure: variance of per-context agreement, context-shuffle null
    p_context = var_context = float("nan")
    if n_scored >= 2 and co_s:
        groups: dict[tuple, dict[str, list]] = {}
        for r in co_s:
            groups.setdefault((r["pair_id"], r["argset"]), {}).setdefault(
                r["context"], []).append(r["agree"])

        def ctx_var(assign: dict[tuple, dict[str, list]]) -> float:
            per_ctx = {c: [] for c in scored}
            for g in assign.values():
                for c, a in g.items():
                    per_ctx[c].extend(a)
            means = [np.mean(v) for v in per_ctx.values() if v]
            return float(np.var(means))

        var_context = ctx_var(groups)
        null_c = np.empty(N_PERM)
        keys = list(groups)
        for k in range(N_PERM):
            shuf = {}
            for key in keys:
                ctxs = list(groups[key])
                perm = rng.permutation(len(ctxs))
                shuf[key] = {ctxs[perm[i]]: groups[key][ctxs[i]]
                             for i in range(len(ctxs))}
            null_c[k] = ctx_var(shuf)
        p_context = _perm_p_greater(var_context, null_c)

    # pre-registered directional contact: A(C1) > A(C2), sign-flip perm
    d_dir = p_dir = float("nan")
    if "C1_direct" in scored and "C2_named" in scored:
        diffs = []
        for pid, ai in {(r["pair_id"], r["argset"]) for r in co_s}:
            c1 = [r["agree"] for r in co_s
                  if r["pair_id"] == pid and r["argset"] == ai
                  and r["context"] == "C1_direct"]
            c2 = [r["agree"] for r in co_s
                  if r["pair_id"] == pid and r["argset"] == ai
                  and r["context"] == "C2_named"]
            if c1 and c2:
                diffs.append(np.mean(c1) - np.mean(c2))
        if diffs:
            diffs_a = np.array(diffs)
            d_dir = float(diffs_a.mean())
            signs = rng.choice([-1.0, 1.0], size=(N_PERM, len(diffs_a)))
            null_d = (signs * np.abs(diffs_a)).mean(axis=1)
            p_dir = _perm_p_greater(d_dir, null_d)

    # bug-taxonomy ADVISORY (never load-bearing; feeds LEDGER-C)
    taxonomy = {"n_divergent": 0, "matches_naive": 0, "matches_weak": 0,
                "lambda_prefix": 0, "other": 0}
    for r in co_s:
        if r["agree"]:
            continue
        taxonomy["n_divergent"] += 1
        for m, ans in ((1, r["ans1"]), (2, r["ans2"])):
            if r[f"correct{m}"]:
                continue
            expr = CONTEXTS[r["context"]][2].format(
                T=r[f"t{m}"], a=ARGSETS[r["argset"]][0],
                b=ARGSETS[r["argset"]][1])
            hit = False
            for name, calc in (("matches_naive", R_NAIVE),
                               ("matches_weak", R_WEAK)):
                nf = _nf(expr, calc)
                if nf is not None and _agree(ans, nf):
                    taxonomy[name] += 1
                    hit = True
                    break
            if not hit:
                if ans.startswith("λ") or ans.startswith("\\"):
                    taxonomy["lambda_prefix"] += 1
                else:
                    taxonomy["other"] += 1

    # frozen verdict tree (exhaustive)
    if not g0_pass or n_scored < MIN_CONTEXTS or not cert_pass:
        verdict = "VOID"
    elif a_coext >= CEIL_BAND:
        verdict = "EXTENSIONAL"
    elif not (d_floor >= FLOOR_D and p_floor < ALPHA):
        verdict = "LEXICAL-FLOOR"
    elif p_context < ALPHA:
        verdict = "RATE-STRUCTURED"
    else:
        verdict = "RATE-UNSTRUCTURED"

    return {
        "verdict": verdict, "g0_pass": g0_pass, "det_dev": det_dev,
        "a_ceil": a_ceil, "a_coext": a_coext, "a_floor": a_floor,
        "n_coext_pairs": n_coext_pairs, "n_floor_pairs": n_floor_pairs,
        "cert_pass": cert_pass, "sensitivity": sens, "scored_contexts": scored,
        "n_scored_contexts": n_scored, "a_coext_by_context": a_coext_ctx,
        "d_floor": d_floor, "p_floor": p_floor, "r_len": r_len,
        "d_floor_partial": d_floor_partial,
        "var_context": var_context, "p_context": p_context,
        "d_dir_c1_c2": d_dir, "p_dir_c1_c2": p_dir,
        "taxonomy": taxonomy,
    }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# planted worlds (through the REAL analyse path)


def _synth(world: str, seed: int = 99) -> list[dict]:
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    coext = [(f"T{i}a", f"T{i}b", int(rng.integers(0, 4))) for i in range(16)]
    floor = [(f"F{i}a", f"F{i}b", int(rng.integers(0, 4))) for i in range(16)]
    agree_ctx = {"C1_direct": True, "C2_named": False, "C3_nested": True,
                 "C4_extra_arg": False, "C5_arg_position": True,
                 "C6_discard": True}
    recs: list[dict] = []

    def rec(kind, pid, t1, t2, ctx, ai, a1, a2, dlen):
        return {"kind": kind, "pair_id": pid, "t1": t1, "t2": t2,
                "context": ctx, "argset": ai, "raw1": a1, "raw2": a2,
                "ans1": a1, "ans2": a2, "agree": a1 == a2,
                "expected1": "x", "expected2": "x",
                "correct1": a1 == "x", "correct2": a2 == "x",
                "dlen_tok": dlen}

    for pid, (t1, t2, dlen) in enumerate(coext):
        for ctx in CONTEXTS:
            for ai in range(2):
                if world == "insensitive":
                    a1 = a2 = "a"
                elif ctx == "C6_discard":
                    a1 = a2 = "a"  # discard context: everyone agrees
                elif world == "extensional":
                    a1 = a2 = "x"
                elif world == "lexical":
                    a1, a2 = f"ans_{t1}", f"ans_{t2}"
                elif world == "rate":
                    a1 = "x"
                    a2 = "x" if agree_ctx[ctx] else f"bug_{t2}"
                elif world == "coin":
                    a1 = "x"
                    a2 = "x" if rng.random() < 0.5 else f"bug_{t2}"
                elif world == "nondet":
                    a1 = a2 = "x"
                else:
                    raise ValueError(world)
                recs.append(rec("coext", f"coext{pid}", t1, t2, ctx, ai,
                                a1, a2, dlen))
    for pid, (t1, t2, dlen) in enumerate(floor):
        for ctx in CONTEXTS:
            for ai in range(2):
                if world == "insensitive" or ctx == "C6_discard":
                    a1 = a2 = "a"
                else:
                    a1, a2 = f"ans_{t1}", f"ans_{t2}"
                recs.append(rec("floor", f"floor{pid}", t1, t2, ctx, ai,
                                a1, a2, dlen))
    for i in range(48):
        bad = world == "nondet" and i % 6 == 0
        recs.append(rec("ceil", f"ceil{i}", "T", "T", "C1_direct", 0,
                        "x", "y" if bad else "x", 0))
    return recs


def run_validate() -> int:
    # A4 extraction self-test (chain, junk, enumeration)
    assert _extract_answer("W K a = K a a = a\nfoo") == "a"
    assert _extract_answer("1\nI b = 2") == "1"
    assert _extract_answer(" a b ") == "a b"
    log("--validate: 6 planted worlds through the REAL analyse path")
    expect = {
        "extensional": "EXTENSIONAL",
        "lexical": "LEXICAL-FLOOR",
        "rate": "RATE-STRUCTURED",
        "coin": "RATE-UNSTRUCTURED",
        "nondet": "VOID",
        "insensitive": "VOID",
    }
    fails = 0
    for world, want in expect.items():
        st = analyse(_synth(world), seed=7)
        got = st["verdict"]
        ok = got == want
        fails += 0 if ok else 1
        log(f"  {'✓' if ok else '✗'} {world:12s} want {want:17s} got {got:17s} "
            f"(A_co {st['a_coext']:.2f} A_fl {st['a_floor']:.2f} "
            f"D {st['d_floor']:.2f} p {st['p_floor']:.3f} "
            f"pctx {st['p_context']:.3f} scored {st['n_scored_contexts']})")
    log(f"validate: {6 - fails}/6")
    return 1 if fails else 0


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--validate", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--smoke", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--model-id", default="Qwen/Qwen3-14B")
    ap.add_argument("--out", default=None)
    args = ap.parse_args()

    if args.validate:
        return run_validate()

    # smoke >= 4B, prefer 7B+ (Michael s347): the calculus function is not
    # fully formed below ~4B (s345 scar: 0.6B smoke degenerated the register)
    model_id = "Qwen/Qwen3-8B" if args.smoke and args.model_id == "Qwen/Qwen3-14B" \
        else args.model_id
    n_coext, n_floor, n_ceil = (8, 8, 24) if args.smoke else (N_COEXT, N_FLOOR, N_CEIL)

    corpus = build_corpus(n_coext, n_floor, SEED)
    corpus_hash = hashlib.sha256(
        json.dumps({k: corpus[k] for k in ("coext", "floor")},
                   sort_keys=True).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
    cap = capture(model_id, corpus, n_ceil, SEED)
    # A6: certification floor scales with corpus target (smoke can pass)
    stats = analyse(cap["records"], min_pairs=max(4, n_coext // 2))

    tag = "run_smoke" if args.smoke else "run_14b"
    out = Path(args.out) if args.out else _ROOT / "results" / "p_obs_equiv_s347" / tag
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with (out / "results.jsonl").open("w") as f:
        for r in cap["records"]:
            f.write(json.dumps(r, default=_json_native) + "\n")
    meta = {
        "run_id": f"p_obs_equiv_s347/{tag}",
        "timestamp": datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(),
        "model": model_id, "sampling": {"strategy": "greedy", "n": DECODE_N},
        "git_sha": git_sha(), "corpus_hash": corpus_hash, "seed": SEED,
        "n_perm": N_PERM, "frozen": "fab97fed",
        "corpus": {"coext": corpus["coext"], "floor": corpus["floor"],
                   "excluded": corpus["excluded"]},
        "driver_validity": cap["validity"],
        "stats": stats,
    }
    (out / "meta.json").write_text(json.dumps(meta, indent=2, default=_json_native))
    log(f"VERDICT {stats['verdict']} | A_ceil {stats['a_ceil']:.3f} "
        f"A_coext {stats['a_coext']:.3f} A_floor {stats['a_floor']:.3f} | "
        f"D {stats['d_floor']:.3f} p {stats['p_floor']:.4f} | "
        f"pctx {stats['p_context']:.4f} | dir C1>C2 {stats['d_dir_c1_c2']:.3f} "
        f"p {stats['p_dir_c1_c2']:.4f} | scored {stats['scored_contexts']}")
    log(f"wrote {out}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

    def _register(self, ids: list[int], text: str, cache, logits) -> Seal:
        s = Seal(self._next_sid, list(ids), text, cache, logits)
        self.seals[s.sid] = s
        self._next_sid += 1
        return s

    def prefill(self, text: str, chat: bool = False) -> Seal:
        """Compile text onto the tape: one forward, returns an immutable Seal."""
        if chat:
            text = self.tok.apply_chat_template(
                [{"role": "user", "content": text}],
                tokenize=False,
                add_generation_prompt=True,
                enable_thinking=False,
            )
        ids = self.tok(text, return_tensors="pt").input_ids[0].tolist()
        cache = DynamicCache()
        out = self._forward(ids, cache, hidden=False, attn=False)
        logits = out.logits[0, -1].detach().float().cpu()
        return self._register(ids, text, cache, logits)

    def bounce(
        self,
        src: str | Seal,
        n: int = 32,
        hidden: bool = True,
        attn: bool = False,
        stop_at_eos: bool = True,
        keep_seal: bool = True,
    ) -> Bounce:
        """Greedy-decode n tokens from text or a Seal, capturing per-emission state.

        A Seal's cache is CLONED before use (append law) — the seal survives.
        """
        signs, hiddens, attns, new_ids, toks = [], [], [], [], []
        if isinstance(src, Seal):
            cache = _clone_cache(src.cache)
            ids = list(src.ids)
            text = src.text
            logits = src.logits_last.clone()
            # frame 0 (the deciding state of the sealed position) is not
            # re-run; captures start at the first step forward. To get frame
            # 0 captures, bounce from text instead.
            first_frame_pending = False
        else:
            text = src
            ids = self.tok(src, return_tensors="pt").input_ids[0].tolist()
            cache = DynamicCache()
            out = self._forward(ids, cache, hidden=hidden, attn=attn)
            logits = out.logits[0, -1].detach().float().cpu()
            signs.append(self._grab_signs())
            if hidden:
                hiddens.append(self._frame_hidden(out))
            if attn:
                attns.append(self._frame_attn(out))
            first_frame_pending = True

        for _k in range(n):
            nxt = int(torch.argmax(logits).item())
            new_ids.append(nxt)
            toks.append(self.tok.decode([nxt]))
            if stop_at_eos and nxt in self._eos:
                break
            out = self._forward([nxt], cache, hidden=hidden, attn=attn)
            logits = out.logits[0, -1].detach().float().cpu()
            signs.append(self._grab_signs())
            if hidden:
                hiddens.append(self._frame_hidden(out))
            if attn:
                attns.append(self._frame_attn(out))

        # align: frame k emitted token k; drop the trailing frame (it decides
        # the (n+1)th token, which we did not take).
        n_emit = len(new_ids)
        if first_frame_pending:
            frames = signs[:n_emit]
            hframes = hiddens[:n_emit] if hidden else None
            aframes = attns[:n_emit] if attn else None
        else:  # seal path: frame k-1 (step forwards) emitted token k; frame
            # for token 0 lives in the seal's pending logits (no capture).
            frames = signs[: max(n_emit - 1, 0)]
            hframes = hiddens[: max(n_emit - 1, 0)] if hidden else None
            aframes = attns[: max(n_emit - 1, 0)] if attn else None

        end_seal = None
        if keep_seal:
            end_seal = self._register(
                ids + new_ids, text + "".join(toks), cache, logits
            )
        return Bounce(
            prompt_text=text,
            prompt_ids=ids,
            new_ids=new_ids,
            tokens=toks,
            signs=np.stack(frames)
            if frames
            else np.zeros((0, self.n_layers, 0), dtype=np.int8),
            hidden=np.stack(hframes) if hidden and hframes else None,
            attn=aframes if attn else None,
            end_seal=end_seal,
        )

    def fork(self, seal: Seal, alt_text: str = "", n: int = 32, **kw) -> Bounce:
        """Branch a sealed continuation: append alt_text (may be empty), decode n.

        fork(seal, "") is the identity plant — must reproduce bounce(seal).
        """
        if not alt_text:
            return self.bounce(seal, n=n, **kw)
        cache = _clone_cache(seal.cache)
        alt_ids = (
            self.tok(alt_text, add_special_tokens=False, return_tensors="pt")
            .input_ids[0]
            .tolist()
        )
        out = self._forward(alt_ids, cache, hidden=False, attn=False)
        logits = out.logits[0, -1].detach().float().cpu()
        branched = self._register(
            seal.ids + alt_ids, seal.text + alt_text, cache, logits
        )
        return self.bounce(branched, n=n, **kw)

    def drop_seal(self, sid: int) -> None:
        self.seals.pop(sid, None)

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- views

    def _load_pole_frame(self, order: list[str] | None = None) -> None:
        z = np.load(_CENTROIDS_NPZ)
        basis = [str(b) for b in z["basis"]]
        order = order or [s for s in BASIS17 if s in basis]
        missing = [s for s in (order or []) if s not in basis]
        if missing:
            raise ValueError(f"states not in committed basis: {missing}")
        idx = [basis.index(s) for s in order]
        cent = z["centroids"][:, idx, :].astype(np.float32)  # [L, S, d]
        mu = cent.mean(axis=1)  # [L, d]
        centc = cent - mu[:, None, :]
        nrm = np.linalg.norm(centc, axis=2, keepdims=True)
        self._pole_P = centc / np.where(nrm < 1e-9, 1.0, nrm)
        self._pole_mu = mu
        self._pole_order = order

    def routes(self, b: Bounce | np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Per-emission pole cosines [n, L, S] against the committed 17-frame."""
        if self._pole_P is None:
            self._load_pole_frame()
        signs = b.signs if isinstance(b, Bounce) else b
        x = signs.astype(np.float32) - self._pole_mu[None]
        nrm = np.linalg.norm(x, axis=2, keepdims=True)
        xn = x / np.where(nrm < 1e-9, 1.0, nrm)
        return np.einsum("nld,lsd->nls", xn, self._pole_P).astype(np.float32)

    def stations(self, b: Bounce, band: tuple[int, int] | None = None) -> list[str]:
        """Per-token argmax pole averaged over a layer band (default: top 25%)."""
        r = self.routes(b)
        lo, hi = band or (int(self.n_layers * 0.75), self.n_layers)
        top = r[:, lo:hi, :].mean(axis=1)  # [n, S]
        out = []
        for k in range(top.shape[0]):
            s = int(np.argmax(top[k]))
            out.append(f"{b.tokens[k]!r} → {self._pole_order[s]} ({top[k, s]:+.3f})")
        return out

    def lens(self, b: Bounce, step: int = -1, layer: int = -1, top_k: int = 8):
        """Logit-lens the deciding state of emission `step` at `layer`."""
        from verbum.jlens import logit_lens

        if b.hidden is None:
            raise ValueError("bounce captured no hidden states (hidden=False)")
        h = torch.from_numpy(b.hidden[step, layer].astype(np.float32))
        logits = logit_lens(self.model, h)
        idx = torch.topk(logits, top_k).indices.tolist()
        return [self.tok.decode([i]) for i in idx]

    def read_mass(self, b: Bounce, step: int = -1) -> np.ndarray:
        """[L, T_k] head-averaged attention of emission `step` over the tape."""
        if b.attn is None:
            raise ValueError("bounce captured no attention (attn=False)")
        return b.attn[step].astype(np.float32)

    def _mk_hook(self, li: int):
        def hook(_m, _inp, out):
            v = out[0, -1].detach().float().cpu().numpy()  # emitting position
            self._sign_buf[li] = np.sign(v).astype(np.int8)

        return hook

    def _grab_signs(self) -> np.ndarray:
        return np.stack([self._sign_buf[li] for li, _, _ in self._gates])  # [L, d]

    # ------------------------------------------------------------- forwards

    @torch.no_grad()
    def _forward(self, ids: list[int], cache: DynamicCache, hidden: bool, attn: bool):
        t = torch.tensor([ids], device=self.device)
        return self.model(
            input_ids=t,
            past_key_values=cache,
            use_cache=True,
            output_hidden_states=hidden,
            output_attentions=attn,
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _frame_hidden(out) -> np.ndarray:
        return np.stack(
            [h[0, -1].detach().float().cpu().numpy() for h in out.hidden_states]
        ).astype(np.float16)  # [L+1, d]

    @staticmethod
    def _frame_attn(out) -> np.ndarray:
        rows = [
            a[0, :, -1, :].mean(0).detach().float().cpu().numpy()
            for a in out.attentions
        ]
        return np.stack(rows).astype(np.float16)  # [L, T_k]

I need to run this with `uv run` since torch isn't in base python. For the unembedding, I'll use `model.lm_head.weight` to get the vocab-by-d matrix, and for the answer axis geometry I'll take the raw lm_head row for the answer token and compute its cosine similarity against the plane axis, matching how the earlier memory computed that 0.152 value.

I've confirmed the hidden states tensor is 41×5120, covering the embedding layer plus all 40 transformer layers. Now I'm structuring the harness to mirror the earlier obs_equiv.py pattern: frozen constants, a battery of prompts, a capture function that records the hidden-state trajectory and output token per prompt, an analyse function computing metrics against null distributions and synthetic planted-world baselines, plus validation and main entry points. I'm weighing how to store the data — keeping the raw hidden states in a compact npz file separate from the per-prompt scalar metrics in results.jsonl, while making sure capture and analyse stay cleanly decoupled.

Analyse should consume hidden-state arrays and token ids to compute all metrics, nulls, and verdicts, with planted worlds feeding synthetic arrays into that same analyse function so the plumbing is identical for real and synthetic data. For the answer-axis null, analyse also needs the answer token's unembedding vector and a sample of random-token unembedding rows, which capture will save alongside the hidden states — letting planted worlds synthesize these vectors directly without needing a real model.

Each record per pid carries the hidden-state array, the answer-axis unembed vector, and metadata like task, prompt, and emission status, with a shared random unembed matrix drawn once for all pids (or synthesized as Gaussian noise for planted worlds). Analyse then takes these records plus the random matrix and produces per-pid metrics, runs the null tests, and aggregates everything into gates and a final verdict — I'm now working out the details of that per-pid computation.

For each pid I center the hidden states, then extract the dominant oscillatory plane by building shifted data matrices and running a reduced DMD to get eigenvalues, picking the leading complex pair with phase beyond a small threshold, lifting its eigenvector back to full dimension, and orthonormalizing the real and imaginary parts into a 2D plane basis. I realize the DMD helper only returns the reduced operator and projection matrix, not eigenvectors directly, so I need to compute the eigendecomposition myself and extract the leading eigenvector from there.

I'm extracting the spatial mode vector using Ur and the eigenvector of A_tilde, with a fallback to SVD-based plane selection when no eigenvalue has sufficient phase—using the top-2 right singular vectors of the Hankel matrix as the projection basis when needed.

Now I'm defining the metrics computed from this projection: amplitude and angle per layer, a charging band detection based on amplitude thresholds, coherence and rotation rate over that band, alignment of the basis vectors with the answer direction, and an amplitude charge ratio across the band.

For null testing, I'm considering shuffling layer order and recomputing the plane and coherence each time, but realizing that re-extracting the plane per draw across thousands of permutations and many problem IDs would require an impractically large number of DMD computations, so I need a cheaper approach.

The most tractable option is to extract the plane once from the real trajectory, then project each null trajectory onto that fixed plane to compute coherence, which tests whether the observed alignment could arise by chance given the same reference plane.

For the shuffled-layer and increment-shuffle nulls, I project the shuffled data onto the real plane and compute R_coh directly. The norm-matched random-plane null is trickier since it asks whether matched charging magnitudes with isotropic random directions could manufacture this coherence on their own, so I need to build a synthetic trajectory with the same amplitude profile but random directions and figure out whether to project it onto its own best-fit plane or measure the maximum coherence across random planes.

Since this null needs to re-extract the plane via DMD/SVD on norm-matched noise, it's the critical test of whether the plane-extraction step itself could be manufacturing coherence from norm growth alone. I'll use 200 permutations for the plane-re-extracting nulls (N1-N3), which keeps the DMD computation manageable, while the answer-axis null stays cheap since it's just cosine comparisons.

For G0, I'm defining pass as valid determinant plus enough pids with recoverable planes and sufficient charging, excluding failing pids and voiding if too few survive. For G1, I'm setting per-pid pass as coherence exceeding the 95th percentile across all three nulls, then aggregating via a binomial test comparing the observed pass fraction against chance level.

For G2, I'm comparing alignment scores against a random-token cosine null using the same binomial approach, reporting median alignment. For G3, I'm computing the coefficient of variation in correlation across task types and testing it against a shuffled-label null, passing only if the real dispersion falls below the 5th percentile of that null.

I also need to settle the rotation direction issue — since dtheta can be positive or negative, I'll use concentration-based coherence per pid (sign-agnostic) but take the absolute value of the precession rate for the cross-pid consistency metric. Now I'm setting up the synthetic world generator, building records with pid, task, hidden states, answer vectors, and validity flags, plus a random comparison set, using a small embedding dimension for tractability.

I'm defining three synthetic world types: pure rotation with growing charge in a fixed 2D plane (should trigger CHARGED-ROTATION), norm growth with no rotation and random answer axis (should fail coherence and register as GENERIC), and off-axis rotation where the answer axis is orthogonal to the rotation plane (should pass G1 but fail G2, landing as MIXED).

I'm also adding drift (random-direction increments with matched norms, expected GENERIC) and degenerate (flat noise with no charging, expected VOID since amplitude never grows 4x). Now I'm stress-testing whether these planted worlds actually produce their intended verdicts under the real analysis — the norm_growth case worries me, since a constant theta with zero angular change could spuriously produce a high coherence ratio rather than the low one I want.

I realize the concentration metric alone can't distinguish true coherent rotation from a constant angle with no rotation at all — both give perfect concentration. The fix needs to require a meaningfully non-zero median rotation angle in addition to high concentration, since norm_growth has essentially zero rotation and should fail that check.

For the ray case, the dominant axis captures the direction and the secondary axis is just noise, so the angle stays roughly constant and the swept angle is near zero — a good discriminator. This means I need a rotation score combining both total swept angle and coherence of the angular increments, requiring both to be substantial simultaneously rather than either alone.

= np.linalg.norm(U_rand, axis=1)\n            cos = np.abs(U_rand @ B.T) / (nu[:, None] * np.linalg.norm(B, axis=1)[None] + EPS)\n            null4 = cos.max(axis=1)\n            rec[\"N4_q95\"] = float(np.quantile(null4, 0.95))\n            rec[\"beats_N4\"] = bool(a_align > rec[\"N4_q95\"])\n        per.append(rec)\n\n    valid = [p for p in per if p.get(\"valid\")]\n    n_valid = len(valid)\n    per_task_counts = {t: sum(1 for p in valid if p[\"task\"] == t) for t in TASKS}\n    tasks_ok = sum(1 for c in per_task_counts.values() if c >= min_per_task)\n    g0_pass = bool(det_ok) and n_valid >= min_total and tasks_ok >= 2\n\n    def _gate(key: str) -> tuple[int, float, bool]:\n        nb = sum(1 for p in valid if p.get(key))\n        pval = binom_sf(nb, n_valid, ALPHA) if n_valid else 1.0\n        return nb, pval, pval < ALPHA\n\n    n1 = _gate(\"beats_N1\"); n2 = _gate(\"beats_N2\"); n3 = _gate(\"beats_N3\")\n    g1_pass = g0_pass and n1[2] and n2[2] and n3[2]\n    n4 = _gate(\"beats_N4\")\n    g2_pass = g1_pass and n4[2]\n\n    # G3 (qualifier): cross-task consistency of |rho| vs shuffled-task-label null\n    g3_pass = False\n    cv_real = cv_q05 = float(\"nan\")\n    if n_valid >= min_total and tasks_ok >= 2:\n        rhos = np.array([abs(p[\"rho_deg\"]) for p in valid])\n        labs = np.array([p[\"task\"] for p in valid])\n        def _cv(labels):\n            means = [rhos[labels == t].mean() for t in TASKS\n                     if (labels == t).sum() >= min_per_task]\n            means = np.array(means)\n            return float(np.std(means) / (np.mean(means) + EPS)) if len(means) >= 2 else np.nan\n        cv_real = _cv(labs)\n        null_cv = np.array([_cv(rng.permutation(labs)) for _ in range(N_PERM)])\n        null_cv = null_cv[~np.isnan(null_cv)]\n        if not np.isnan(cv_real) and null_cv.size:\n            cv_q05 = float(np.quantile(null_cv, 0.05))\n            g3_pass = cv_real < cv_q05\n\n    if not g0_pass:\n        verdict = \"VOID\"\n    elif not g1_pass:\n        verdict = \"GENERIC-NORM-GROWTH-ONLY\"\n    elif not g2_pass:\n        verdict = \"MIXED\"\n    else:\n        verdict = \"CHARGED-ROTATION\"\n    qualifier = (\"universal\" if g3_pass else \"per-task\") if verdict == \"CHARGED-ROTATION\" else None\n\n    med_align = float(np.median([p[\"a_align\"] for p in valid])) if valid else float(\"nan\")\n    med_rot = float(np.median([p[\"ROT\"] for p in valid])) if valid else float(\"nan\")\n    med_rho = float(np.median([p[\"rho_deg\"] for p in valid])) if valid else float(\"nan\")\n    med_charge = float(np.median([p[\"charge\"] for p in valid])) if valid else float(\"nan\")\n    return {\n        \"verdict\": verdict, \"qualifier\": qualifier, \"g0_pass\": g0_pass,\n        \"g1_pass\": g1_pass, \"g2_pass\": g2_pass, \"g3_pass\": g3_pass,\n        \"det_ok\": bool(det_ok), \"n_valid\": n_valid, \"n_records\": len(per),\n        \"per_task_valid\": per_task_counts, \"tasks_ok\": tasks_ok,\n        \"N1\": {\"n_beat\": n1[0], \"p\": n1[1]}, \"N2\": {\"n_beat\": n2[0], \"p\": n2[1]},\n        \"N3\": {\"n_beat\": n3[0], \"p\": n3[1]}, \"N4\": {\"n_beat\": n4[0], \"p\": n4[1]},\n        \"cv_rho_real\": cv_real, \"cv_rho_q05\": cv_q05,\n        \"median_a_align\": med_align, \"median_ROT\": med_rot,\n        \"median_rho_deg\": med_rho, \"median_charge\": med_charge,\n        \"per_pid\": per,\n    }\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# capture (driver — depth trajectory of the deciding state)\n\n\ndef capture(model_id: str, tasks: dict, seed: int) -> dict:\n    from verbum.driver import Driver\n\n    d = Driver(model_id=model_id)\n    validity = d.validity()\n    log(f\"driver validity: {validity}\")\n    W = d.model.lm_head.weight.detach().float().cpu().numpy()  # (vocab, d)\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    U_rand = W[rng.choice(W.shape[0], size=N_RAND_TOK, replace=False)].astype(np.float32)\n\n    records, arrays = [], {}\n    t0 = time.time()\n    for task, items in tasks.items():\n        for j, (prompt, expected) in enumerate(items):\n            b = d.bounce(prompt, n=DECODE_N, hidden=True, keep_seal=False)\n            if b.hidden is None or len(b.tokens) == 0:\n                continue\n            k = next((i for i, t in enumerate(b.tokens) if t.strip()), None)\n            if k is None:\n                continue\n            H = b.hidden[k].astype(np.float32)  # (L+1, d)\n            tok_id = int(b.new_ids[k])\n            u_ans = W[tok_id].astype(np.float32)\n            gen = \"\".join(b.tokens).strip()\n            pid = f\"{task}{j}\"\n            records.append({\n                \"pid\": pid, \"task\": task, \"prompt\": prompt, \"expected\": expected,\n                \"answer_token\": b.tokens[k], \"answer_token_id\": tok_id,\n                \"generated\": gen, \"correct\": gen.startswith(expected),\n                \"H\": H, \"u_ans\": u_ans,\n            })\n            arrays[f\"H_{pid}\"] = H\n            arrays[f\"u_{pid}\"] = u_ans\n            log(f\"{pid}: {prompt[-24:]!r} -> {b.tokens[k]!r} \"\n                f\"[{time.time() - t0:.0f}s]\")\n    arrays[\"U_rand\"] = U_rand\n    return {\"records\": records, \"validity\": validity, \"arrays\": arrays,\n            \"U_rand\": U_rand}\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# planted worlds (through the REAL analyse path)\n\n\ndef _synth(world: str, d: int = 48, L: int = 40, n_per_task: int = 6,\n           seed: int = 99) -> tuple[list[dict], np.ndarray]:\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    ell = np.arange(L + 1)\n    amp = 0.3 * (1.15 ** ell)          # geometric charge\n    amp_flat = 0.3 * np.ones(L + 1)    # degenerate: no charge\n    records = []\n    for task in TASKS:\n        for j in range(n_per_task):\n            Q, _ = np.linalg.qr(rng.standard_normal((d, 3)))\n            e0, e1, e2 = Q[:, 0], Q[:, 1], Q[:, 2]\n            noise = 0.02 * rng.standard_normal((L + 1, d))\n            if world == \"pure_rotation\":\n                th = np.deg2rad(5.0) * ell\n                H = amp[:, None] * (np.cos(th)[:, None] * e0 + np.sin(th)[:, None] * e1)\n                H = H + noise\n                u_ans = e0.copy()\n            elif world == \"norm_growth\":\n                H = amp[:, None] * e0[None, :] + noise\n                u_ans = e2.copy()\n            elif world == \"rotation_off_axis\":\n                th = np.deg2rad(5.0) * ell\n                H = amp[:, None] * (np.cos(th)[:, None] * e0 + np.sin(th)[:, None] * e1)\n                H = H + noise\n                u_ans = e2.copy()  # answer axis orthogonal to the rotation plane\n            elif world == \"drift\":\n                steps = np.diff(amp)[:, None] * 0 + 1.0  # unit-ish; matched norms below\n                dirs = rng.standard_normal((L, d))\n                dirs /= np.linalg.norm(dirs, axis=1, keepdims=True) + EPS\n                stepn = np.abs(np.diff(amp))[:, None]\n                H = np.vstack([amp[0] * e0, amp[0] * e0 + np.cumsum(dirs * stepn, 0)])\n                H = H + noise\n                u_ans = e2.copy()\n            elif world == \"degenerate\":\n                H = amp_flat[:, None] * e0[None, :] + noise\n                u_ans = e2.copy()\n            else:\n                raise ValueError(world)\n            records.append({\"pid\": f\"{task}{j}\", \"task\": task,\n                            \"H\": H.astype(np.float32), \"u_ans\": u_ans.astype(np.float32)})\n    U_rand = rng.standard_normal((N_RAND_TOK, d)).astype(np.float32)\n    return records, U_rand\n\n\ndef run_validate() -> int:\n    log(\"--validate: 5 planted worlds through the REAL analyse path\")\n    expect = {\n        \"pure_rotation\": \"CHARGED-ROTATION\",\n        \"norm_growth\": \"GENERIC-NORM-GROWTH-ONLY\",\n        \"rotation_off_axis\": \"MIXED\",\n        \"drift\": \"GENERIC-NORM-GROWTH-ONLY\",\n        \"degenerate\": \"VOID\",\n    }\n    fails = 0\n    for world, want in expect.items():\n        recs, U_rand = _synth(world)\n        st = analyse(recs, U_rand, seed=7, n_perm_plane=100,\n                     min_total=20, min_per_task=4)\n        got = st[\"verdict\"]\n        ok = got == want\n        fails += 0 if ok else 1\n        log(f\"  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} {world:18s} want {want:24s} got {got:24s} \"\n            f\"(nvalid {st['n_valid']} ROT {st['median_ROT']:.4f} \"\n            f\"align {st['median_a_align']:.3f} \"\n            f\"N3 {st['N3']['n_beat']}/p{st['N3']['p']:.3f} \"\n            f\"N4 {st['N4']['n_beat']}/p{st['N4']['p']:.3f})\")\n    log(f\"validate: {5 - fails}/5\")\n    return 1 if fails else 0\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--validate\", action=\"store_true\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--smoke\", action=\"store_true\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--model-id\", default=\"Qwen/Qwen3-14B\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--out\", default=None)\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    if args.validate:\n        return run_validate()\n\n    model_id = args.model_id\n    if args.smoke and model_id == \"Qwen/Qwen3-14B\":\n        model_id = \"Qwen/Qwen3-8B\"  # A2 law (s347): smoke >= 4B, prefer 7B+\n    tasks = ({t: items[:4] for t, items in TASKS.items()} if args.smoke else TASKS)\n    min_total = 10 if args.smoke else MIN_TOTAL\n\n    cap = capture(model_id, tasks, SEED)\n    det_ok = bool(cap[\"validity\"].get(\"ok\", False))\n    stats = analyse(cap[\"records\"], cap[\"U_rand\"], seed=SEED,\n                    min_total=min_total, det_ok=det_ok)\n\n    tag = \"run_smoke\" if args.smoke else \"run_14b\"\n    out = Path(args.out) if args.out else _ROOT / \"results\" / \"p_depth_carrier_s348\" / tag\n    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    with (out / \"results.jsonl\").open(\"w\") as f:\n        for r in cap[\"records\"]:\n            row = {k: v for k, v in r.items() if k not in (\"H\", \"u_ans\")}\n            f.write(json.dumps(row, default=_json_native) + \"\\n\")\n    np.savez_compressed(out / \"trajectories.npz\", **cap[\"arrays\"])\n    meta = {\n        \"run_id\": f\"p_depth_carrier_s348/{tag}\",\n        \"timestamp\": datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(),\n        \"model\": model_id, \"sampling\": {\"strategy\": \"greedy\", \"n\": DECODE_N},\n        \"git_sha\": git_sha(), \"seed\": SEED, \"frozen\": \"c953705d\",\n        \"n_perm_plane\": N_PERM_PLANE, \"n_perm\": N_PERM,\n        \"n_prompts\": sum(len(v) for v in tasks.values()),\n        \"driver_validity\": cap[\"validity\"],\n        \"stats\": {k: v for k, v in stats.items() if k != \"per_pid\"},\n        \"per_pid\": stats[\"per_pid\"],\n    }\n    (out / \"meta.json\").write_text(json.dumps(meta, indent=2, default=_json_native))\n    q = f\"/{stats['qualifier']}\" if stats[\"qualifier\"] else \"\"\n    log(f\"VERDICT {stats['verdict']}{q} | g0 {stats['g0_pass']} g1 {stats['g1_pass']} \"\n        f\"g2 {stats['g2_pass']} g3 {stats['g3_pass']} | nvalid {stats['n_valid']} | \"\n        f\"N1 {stats['N1']['n_beat']} N2 {stats['N2']['n_beat']} \"\n        f\"N3 {stats['N3']['n_beat']} N4 {stats['N4']['n_beat']} | \"\n        f\"med ROT {stats['median_ROT']:.4f} align {stats['median_a_align']:.3f} \"\n        f\"rho {stats['median_rho_deg']:.2f} charge {stats['median_charge']:.1f}\")\n    log(f\"wrote {out}\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main())\n"
}

### Thinking

I'm digging into why norm_growth is coming back invalid—the charge ratio is falling below the threshold of 4. I'm tracing through how the amplitude gets computed: the signal is amp*e0 plus noise, then DC-centered and projected onto the extracted plane, and I suspect the issue is in how that projection interacts with the plane's axis when the signal is purely along e0.

Working through the math, since the amplitude grows geometrically, the mean gets pulled heavily toward the large late-layer values—so much that the mean actually exceeds the final amplitude itself. That flips the sign of the DC-centered values for most layers, making the start-to-end ratio come out much smaller than expected, which is exactly why charge drops below 4 and the check fails.

This points to a real conceptual problem: DC-centering distorts the growth measurement for a ray-like signal, since the centered amplitude isn't monotonic even though the raw amplitude is. I'm concluding that charge should probably be measured on the raw amplitude norm rather than the DC-centered one.

So I'll split the measurement: use raw norm growth for the charge/validity check, but keep the DC-centered in-plane coordinates for rotation, since rotation is naturally defined around the DC point. Computing charge from raw norm instead gives a ratio around 138, which clears the threshold comfortably.

Looking at the rotation side, the DC-centered ray stays essentially fixed along one axis with negligible angular drift and near-zero planarity, so the rotation score comes out close to zero — meaning it doesn't outcompete the other candidate and the case stays classified as generic, which checks out.

For pure_rotation though, the test still fails: the rotation score of 0.0266 beats all 30 random-plane draws, meaning the norm-matched random walk never reaches that level. I think this is because the real increments grow geometrically in magnitude, so the random walk's steps are dominated by the last few large ones, skewing the comparison.

The real issue is that geometrically-growing steps inherently make any trajectory dominated by the last few layers, so both the actual data and my null models would show apparent structure concentrated there. Since the real charge growth rate matches this same geometric pattern over ~35 layers, I need to reconsider whether the pilot's coherent precession was actually measured on normalized coordinates rather than raw space.

Since theta itself is amplitude-independent (atan2 doesn't care about radius), the angle measurement should be fair across layers regardless of the geometric growth — the real problem is that equal weighting in R and mean_rate lets the noisy near-origin early points contribute as much as the stable late points.

Working through pure_rotation's early points, I realize DC-centering subtracts a mean vector dominated by the large late-amplitude points, so small early vectors collapse toward a fixed point near -mean rather than spiraling cleanly around the origin — meaning the DC-centered trajectory isn't the clean spiral I assumed, even though the pilot claimed it survives DC-centering.

My synthetic data's huge dynamic range is likely distorting this, so real data may behave differently, or the pilot measures rotation another way. I should step back and think about a more robust metric — something based on normalized per-layer direction vectors rather than raw positions, so the rotation measurement isn't thrown off by amplitude scaling.

Actually, the most defensible approach given the geometric charging issue is to use the DMD eigenvalue's phase directly, since that's literally the per-step rotation rate of the dominant mode — if the leading eigenpair shows a consistent phase with high magnitude and low residual, that's strong evidence of genuine precession.

For norm growth, the leading eigenvalue should be real with zero phase, while random walks lack any dominant coherent eigenpair and show high residual. I'm thinking the metric should combine the rotation rate from the leading complex eigenvalue's angle with the fraction of trajectory energy it explains, distinguishing it from the null cases where no complex eigenpair exists at all.

Rather than overcomplicate this, I'll settle on a robust amplitude-weighted circular concentration statistic: weight each layer-to-layer phase increment by the smaller of its two endpoint radii, so near-origin noise and early collapse don't distort the rotation estimate, then compute the resultant vector's magnitude and angle to get both the concentration and mean rotation rate.

This should cleanly separate the cases: pure rotation keeps high-radius transitions consistent (high weighted concentration, ~5° rate), norm-growth rays have near-zero rotation at high radius (weighted concentration high but rate ≈0), and both random-walk variants show scattered high-radius transitions that drag the weighted concentration down — since the weighting deliberately focuses on where the signal actually lives.

I want to implement this amplitude-weighted circular statistics approach and re-run validation, making sure the charge calculation uses the raw (uncentered) norm, and double-check whether DC-centering distorts planarity for the pure-rotation case given its outward spiral.

I'm computing the weighted rotation coherence and mean rate from the complex sum, then combining with planarity for the final ROT score. I'm also setting validity thresholds requiring charge_raw ≥4 and sufficient band length, then checking test cases: the degenerate flat-amplitude case correctly fails validity and voids, while the norm-growth case passes with near-zero rotation, confirming it's generic.

For the drift case, I'm building the cumulative sum from step differences so raw_norm grows enough to pass the charge threshold, while random rotation keeps ROT low, correctly classifying it as generic. Pure and off-axis cases should pass validity with high ROT, with alignment distinguishing mixed from charged outcomes. Now I'm moving to implement the weighted statistics computation and rewrite the metric section accordingly.

But pure_rotation is still failing—the norm-matched random-plane null keeps reaching the real rotation value, so I need to dig into why that baseline is so strong.

I think I see the culprit: the geometric weighting scheme concentrates almost all weight on the final transition since it's by far the largest, which means R_w collapses to essentially a single term and becomes ≈1 regardless of coherence. That lets even a random walk's last (arbitrary) angle dominate the whole rotation score, explaining why N3 can match the real ROT—the weighting is too aggressive and needs to be spread more evenly across transitions.

I'm considering normalizing each layer's in-plane coordinate to unit length before computing theta, so every layer contributes its direction equally rather than being dominated by amplitude, then equal-weighting all transitions instead of using geometric weights.

Actually with DC-centering, early layers collapse toward a constant vector (the negative mean direction) since their amplitude is tiny relative to the mean, so they don't rotate but stay coherent at a fixed angle. Late layers, with large amplitude, dominate the mean and their direction genuinely rotates. So the unit-direction trajectory transitions from constant early to rotating late, with the real rotational signal concentrated in the later band.

But I recall that even switching to equal weights failed earlier, with the null case still reaching the real signal's rotation score, so I need to figure out why equal weighting alone didn't fix things.

A random walk shouldn't produce coherent rotation under equal weighting since its direction changes are random and should average out. But after centering and extracting the top-2 SVD plane, the walk's principal variance directions might create an illusion of coherent drift, since the SVD naturally aligns with however the walk happened to progress.

This is actually a known artifact — projecting Brownian motion onto its top principal components yields smooth arc-like trajectories (related to Karhunen-Loève expansion), which can masquerade as rotation. That means N3 is a genuinely hard null to beat, and real coherent rotation needs to clearly outperform this apparent-rotation artifact to be distinguishable.

The real discriminator is linearity: real rotation has constant phase rate vs layer (R²≈1), while Brownian's PCA arc has a nonlinear phase curve. But checking the pilot's actual evidence — increment-shuffling killed the real rotation (0/20), yet increment-shuffling a Brownian walk just produces another random walk that still shows a PCA arc, so that test wouldn't distinguish them the way I'd hoped.

For a genuinely clean planted rotation, ROT should exceed Brownian since it's more coherent. The real separator might be that clean rotation holds a constant per-layer rate across the whole trajectory, while Brownian's apparent arc is a boundary artifact tied to sequence length rather than a consistent underlying rate.

A better primary metric could be DMD reconstruction quality: a clean rotation is rank-2 and reconstructs with near-zero residual, while a Brownian walk is effectively full-rank and leaves a high residual. So I'd define rotation quality as the reconstruction fidelity of a rank-2 DMD fit times the phase angle of the leading eigenpair — pure rotation gives near-zero residual and the correct phase, Brownian gives a much higher residual, and a norm-growth ray would show up as a real (non-oscillatory) leading pair.
